# Owner App, Editor and Admin (Plan 4) Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** Build the owner app (invite and magic-link sign-in, a seven-step questionnaire with photo uploads, build progress, an editor with a live in-browser preview, look switching, wording edits, section order and hiding, publish requests and a messages list) and the admin app (Cloudflare Access sign-in, invites, review of the exact stored page with automated checks, approve or reject, take-down and restore, the search-engine switch, owner disable and enable, AI settings), as two Cloudflare Workers that run and are fully tested on this machine with no account or key.

**Architecture:** Two Workers, each a Hono JSON API under `/api/*` plus a React single-page app on Workers Static Assets: `asksite-app` (owners, `apps/app`) and `asksite-admin` (admins behind Cloudflare Access, `apps/admin`). They share `@asksite/app-common` (slug policy, email templates, HTTP conventions), a package with no Cloudflare types so the root typecheck and tests cover it. Both Workers call Plan 2 and Plan 3 only through small port interfaces that restate the design's signatures (§6.4, §7.2, §7.6); every test runs them with local fakes of those contracts inside the real Workers runtime (`createTestHarness`) and in Chromium and WebKit (Playwright with axe) against `vite preview`, Task 24 wires the real packages, with `satisfies` proving they fit, and Task 25 runs the owner's whole journey across the four real Workers in one local runtime. The editor renders the exact page with Plan 1's pure `render()` in a sandboxed `srcdoc` iframe on every valid change.

**Tech Stack:** TypeScript 7.0.2 (strict, no emit), pnpm 10.33.0 workspaces, Node 24.21.0 LTS or 25.6.1 (as Plan 1), Cloudflare Workers with wrangler 4.138.0 (D1, R2, Queues producer, the Images binding, the Rate Limiting binding, Cron Triggers, Static Assets; `createTestHarness` for tests), Hono 4.13.9, jose 6.2.12, Zod 4.6.5, React 19.3.0 and react-dom 19.3.0, Vite 8.3.1 with @cloudflare/vite-plugin 1.59.0, @vitejs/plugin-react 6.1.1, @vitejs/plugin-basic-ssl 2.3.0 and @tailwindcss/vite 4.3.3 (Tailwind 4.3.3), obscenity 0.4.6, Vitest 5.0.1, Playwright 1.63.0 with @axe-core/playwright 4.13.0, sharp 0.35.4 (tests only).

**About "can we use a Hugging Face or other free open-source model instead of Anthropic, and would we have to deploy it?"** Plan 4 never calls a model: the owner app only asks Plan 3's `requestGeneration` for a job, and the admin only shows the settings of whichever model the generator uses. So the answer is Plan 3's and design §6.6's: yes, through Plan 3's `openai-compatible` provider (Cloudflare Workers AI, Groq, Together, OpenRouter or the Hugging Face router), and nothing is deployed; "free" means a free licence, not free computing. For Plan 4 a switch is three values: `MODEL_PROVIDER` and `MODEL_ID` in `apps/admin/wrangler.jsonc`, and `DAILY_MODEL_LIMIT` in `apps/app` and `apps/admin`, must equal the generator's (Plan 3's cross-Worker test enforces it; Task 24 runs it), so the settings page shows that model's worst-case daily cost. The moderator changes them in all three Workers in one commit once the user has chosen the model and limit (cross-plan check §2 item 3); Task 27 only checks them. Which model becomes the default is the user's call after Plan 3's evaluation (design §12.3).

## Global Constraints

Copied from `CLAUDE.md` (the user's rules), the system design `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-24-system-design.md` (binding: its names, types, endpoints, keys and error codes), Plan 1's constraints and the moderator's amendments A1–A6. Every task includes this section.

- No guessing: every version, API and behaviour below was checked on 2026-09-24 against the official source or by running it; claims carry [verified] / [inferred] / [unverified]. Do not add a dependency, option or API without checking it the same way.
- TDD red-then-green: write the failing test, run it and watch it fail for the stated reason, then write the implementation and watch it pass. Never skip the red run.
- KISS and SOLID. One responsibility per file. Clean code. No placeholders.
- No regressions. Plan 1 (`packages/site-schema`, `packages/renderer`, `fixtures/`), Stage 0 (`packages/core`, `packages/site-css`, A6), Plan 2 (`packages/publishing`, `packages/mailer`, `apps/sites`) and Plan 3 (`packages/generation`, `apps/generator`) are consumed, never edited. Plan 4 creates only `packages/app-common/**`, `apps/app/**` and `apps/admin/**`; the only shared file it changes is `pnpm-lock.yaml` (through `pnpm install`). Root files (`package.json`, `tsconfig.json`, `vitest.config.ts`, `pnpm-workspace.yaml`, `.gitignore`) are not touched.
- Contracts: the design's interfaces are binding, as the owning plans amend them (listed in decisions 24 and 29: `restore` takes `ROOT_DOMAIN` and `MEDIA` and returns `missingPhotos`, two more publishing error codes, `ipRateKey`, `worstCaseJobMicrousd` returning `number | null`). The ports in `apps/*/src/worker/deps.ts` restate §6.4, §7.2 and §7.6 with those amendments, as properties with function types so the check is strict; if a real package differs, Task 24's typecheck fails and the moderator decides. No plan edits another plan's package; contract changes go through the moderator.
- Commit messages are 3 words maximum. No `Co-Authored-By`, no "Generated with", no AI attribution of any kind. Commits are made as the repo-local identity `sydashir` <meetashirr@gmail.com> (already configured; never change global git config). Every commit step ends with `git log -1 --format='%an <%ae>%n%B'`, which must print `sydashir <meetashirr@gmail.com>`, then the message and nothing else (no trailer); anything else: stop and fix it before the next task (`git commit --amend --reset-author` on this unpushed branch, never on a pushed one).
- Stage named paths only (`git add <paths>`, as each commit step lists them). Never `git add -A` or `git add .`. The moderating session's uncommitted edits under `docs/` stay unstaged.
- Never push and never run any `gh` command: the moderator pushes.
- Never commit secrets (`.env`, `.env.*`, `.dev.vars`, `*.pem`, `*.key`; all gitignored). Secrets live only in Wrangler secrets (`wrangler secret put`) or the gitignored `.dev.vars`; the committed `.dev.vars.example` files hold names and harmless local values only. Never print a key, never put one in a log, a test, a fixture or chat.
- Exact dependency versions, never `^` or `~`, all checked with `npm view` on 2026-09-24 and again on 2026-09-25 (every pinned version is published; hono, jose, react, react-dom and vite are still the latest; wrangler 4.139.0 and @cloudflare/vite-plugin 1.60.0, which depends on it, were published on 2026-09-24 after the pins, and the plan stays on the design's pair: @cloudflare/vite-plugin 1.59.0 depends on exactly wrangler 4.138.0 [verified: `npm view @cloudflare/vite-plugin@1.59.0 dependencies`]), matching design §11.1: wrangler 4.138.0, hono 4.13.9, jose 6.2.12, zod 4.6.5, react and react-dom 19.3.0, @types/react and @types/react-dom 19.3.0, vite 8.3.1, @vitejs/plugin-react 6.1.1, @cloudflare/vite-plugin 1.59.0, @vitejs/plugin-basic-ssl 2.3.0, @tailwindcss/vite and tailwindcss 4.3.3, vitest 5.0.1, @playwright/test 1.63.0, @axe-core/playwright 4.13.0, typescript 7.0.2, @types/node 24.13.6; plus obscenity 0.4.6 and sharp 0.35.4 (dev). Licences read from the actual LICENSE files: hono, jose, react, react-dom and obscenity MIT; sharp Apache-2.0.
- Every Worker: `"compatibility_date": "2026-09-21"`, no `nodejs_compat`, `"workers_dev": false`, `"preview_urls": false`, `"observability": { "enabled": true, "logs": { "invocation_logs": false } }`; the committed `wrangler.jsonc` holds the PRODUCTION values as plain JSON; a unit test fails the build on unsafe production settings (§9.1). One structured log line per request: route pattern, status, duration, error code. Never tokens, emails, IPs, bodies or keys (§1.2).
- Security (design §2.2, §4.1, §5, §9.1): owner edits follow every Plan 1 copy rule and are escaped like AI copy; text reaches screens only as React text nodes (`dangerouslySetInnerHTML` and any raw-HTML write are banned by a test); any `href` built from stored data passes Plan 1's `isSafeUrl`; previews are `<iframe sandbox srcdoc>` without `allow-scripts` or `allow-same-origin`; stored versions are served with `CSP: sandbox` and `X-Frame-Options: SAMEORIGIN`; every other API response carries `Cache-Control: no-store`, `X-Robots-Tag: noindex`, `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer`, `X-Frame-Options: DENY`, `default-src 'none'` and, in production only, HSTS (Plan 2 decision 8: HSTS from a `localhost` host would force https onto every local project); state-changing requests need `Origin` equal to `APP_ORIGIN` / `ADMIN_ORIGIN` and the expected content type; tokens are 256-bit, single use, stored as SHA-256 hashes, delivered in URL fragments and spent only by a button press; sessions use a `__Host-` HttpOnly Secure SameSite=Lax cookie backed by D1; another owner's site is always 404; exact D1 counts enforce every money or safety cap.
- Accessibility (WCAG 2.2 AA, design §9.2): native controls with visible labels; errors tied with `aria-describedby` and an error summary that takes focus on a failed submit; `autocomplete` tokens; focus moves to the page heading when the page or step changes; autosave status in a polite live region (including failures and conflicts); Move up / Move down buttons, never drag; targets at least 24 × 24 CSS px; nothing asked twice; no password or puzzle login; the preview frame titled "Preview of your website"; native `<dialog>`; `prefers-reduced-motion` respected; reflow at 320 px and 200 % zoom. Proven with axe (tags `wcag2a wcag2aa wcag21a wcag21aa wcag22aa`) on every screen at 390 and 1280 px in Chromium and 390 px in WebKit, a 320 px reflow check, and keyboard-only runs of the owner's questionnaire, editor and publishing and of the admin's review, takedown and settings.
- Everything in Tasks 1–23 runs locally with no account and no key. Tasks 24–26 need Plans 2 and 3 merged (still no account). Only Task 27 needs the user's accounts, keys or devices.
- Work on the branch `plan4-app`, in the git worktree the moderator makes for this plan at `/Users/ashir/Documents/workk2/asksite-plan4`, from `main` after Stage 0 merges (CLAUDE.md: each parallel session works in its own worktree and branch). It sits outside `/Users/ashir/Documents/workk2/web_maker`, where Plan 2B runs on `plan2-hosting`, so Plan 2's leftover-process check and its `pkill -f "/Users/ashir/Documents/workk2/web_maker/"` never match this plan's processes. Run every command from that checkout's root (`cd "$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel)"`), so every check and test sees this plan's own tree. If `main` moved while this branch was built, merge `main` into it first (message "Sync with main") and re-run every check (CLAUDE.md).
- Process hygiene: stop every process you start. Test harnesses close their `workerd` in `afterAll`; Playwright stops its `vite preview`. Never run a bare `killall node` or `pkill node` (the user's other projects run node); target only processes whose command line contains this worktree's path (`/Users/ashir/Documents/workk2/asksite-plan4/`).
- Do not use the Playwright MCP browser tools. Playwright runs only from this repo's dev dependencies.

## Before you start

- Plan 1 is merged (`render`, `escapeText`, `escapeAttr`, `FONTS`, `PALETTES` from `@asksite/renderer`; `fixtures/index.ts`).
- Stage 0 is merged (design §11.3; it is Plan 2 Part A, Plan 2 Tasks 1–6): `@asksite/core` exactly as §2.8, §4.2 and §4.3 write it (entry `./src/index.ts`), `packages/core/migrations/0001_init.sql`, `@asksite/site-css` (`SITE_CSS`, `SITE_CSS_SHA256`), amendment A6 (`HIDEABLE_SECTIONS`, `OwnerHidden`, `SiteDocument.hidden`), `pnpm-workspace.yaml` listing `apps/*`, and the root Vitest `unit`/`workerd` projects. Task 1 Step 5 checks every name Plan 4 uses and stops the plan if one is missing.
- The stylesheet module `packages/site-css/src/generated.ts` is gitignored and only `pnpm build:css` writes it (`pnpm install` does not), so a fresh worktree has none: Task 1 Step 3 runs `pnpm install && pnpm build:css`. Run `pnpm build:css` again whenever Plan 1's styles change (`pnpm typecheck` and `pnpm test` also run it). Without it the contract check fails with `Cannot find module './generated.ts'`, which is this missing build step, not a missing Stage 0 name.
- Until Task 24 writes `apps/*/src/worker/index.ts`, the `main` of both production configs points at a file that does not exist yet, so either app's `build` fails and Plan 2's plain `pnpm dev` (which builds every app first) stops. While Tasks 4–23 run, start the other Workers with `pnpm dev --only sites,generator`; the app and admin run in their own tests.
- Plans 2 and 3 are NOT needed until Task 24. Tasks 1–23 can run in parallel with them. The root `tsconfig.json` and `tsconfig.workers.json` never include `apps/app` or `apps/admin` (Plan 3 adds only `apps/generator`), so each app is type-checked by its own configs; the root `vitest.config.ts` globs of Plan 2 do cover `apps/*`, which is why Plan 4 names its runtime tests `*.workerd.test.ts` (decision 26).
- Browsers: Playwright's Chromium headless shell and WebKit at the revisions @playwright/test 1.63.0 pins (Plan 1 installs them with `pnpm e2e:install`; Task 14 Step 3 installs them if missing).
- Ports 8787 (app) and 8788 (admin) must be free while the browser tests run, and 8789 while Task 25's journey runs (Plan 2's `pnpm dev` uses all three; do not stop it: Plan 2B's `pnpm dev` may belong to another session, so ask the moderator).

## Decisions made while writing this plan

Each was checked by running the code in a scratch replay of this plan unless it says otherwise. Items marked **(moderator)** differ from, or add to, the design text.

1. **Forms are plain controlled React components, not react-hook-form.** **(moderator)** The draft (facts, brief, edits) is the one source of truth: it is saved as the owner types and may be invalid (§2.5), the preview renders from it, a conflict reloads it, and errors come from the same Zod schemas the server uses (`Facts`, `Brief`, `SiteDocument`). react-hook-form keeps its own copy of the values, which would have to be synchronised both ways with the autosaved draft. Plain native inputs keep everything §11.2 wanted from it (labels, `autocomplete`, native behaviour). react-hook-form and @hookform/resolvers are therefore not dependencies.
2. **`@asksite/app-common` holds the code both Workers share** and has no Cloudflare binding types (`RateLimiter` and `SqlDatabase` are structural), so it joins the root typecheck and tests. The admin client reuses the owner client's components by relative import (`apps/admin` → `apps/app/src/client`): one set of accessible building blocks, one visual language.
3. **Ports and fakes for Plans 2 and 3.** Each Worker is built by `createWorker(deps)` / `createAdminWorker(deps, keys)`; `deps.ts` restates the design's signatures; tests use fakes that follow the contracts (`apps/app/test/support/fakes.ts`, `apps/admin/test/support/fakes.ts`); Task 24's `src/worker/index.ts` passes the real packages with `satisfies`. This lets Plan 4 run in parallel with Plans 2 and 3 (design §11.3 "code against the contract").
4. **Web-address blocking is exact; the profanity library only flags.** `slugProblem` blocks an exact brand, bank, platform, government or franchise name (213, in slug form) and 43 whole words; `obscenity` 0.4.6 and brand words inside a slug only flag it for the reviewer, because the library blocks real names (it matches `dickson-electric`, `cumming-hvac`, `cockpit-roofing`, `rapeseed-lawn`) and `chase-hvac` can be an owner's own name. Every site is approved by a human anyway.
5. **Login tokens are deleted by the daily cleanup only once they are a day old**, not when they expire after 15 minutes, so the "10 per owner per day" cap (§5.2) still counts them.
6. **The root domain is baked into each build** (design §9.1: the static `_headers`, whose `img-src` media host the preview inherits, is generated at build time). `build` is therefore the LOCAL build (Vite development mode, which reads `.dev.vars`, falling back to `.dev.vars.example`): that is the script Plan 2's `pnpm dev` runs. `build:production` and `release` use the committed production values, and a test pins the three scripts (`release`, because `pnpm deploy` is a built-in pnpm command that would never run a script named `deploy`, verified with pnpm 10.33.0). **(moderator)** A build also rewrites `.wrangler/deploy/config.json`, which points a bare `wrangler deploy` at the last build (even an e2e one with test fakes: the file was verified, and wrangler's source follows it), so the only deploy path is `pnpm --filter <app> run release`. As a second guard the test configs name their Workers `asksite-app-test`, `asksite-app-e2e`, `asksite-admin-test` and `asksite-admin-e2e`, never a production name, and each app's config test fails if one does: a bare deploy after a test build could create a stray Worker, never replace `asksite-app` or `asksite-admin`.
7. **`assets.directory` is `./dist/client` in the committed config**, so plain `wrangler dev` (Plan 2's `pnpm dev`) and `wrangler deploy` can serve the built client; the Vite plugin rewrites it in its own output (verified). SPA fallback and `run_worker_first: ["/api/*"]` are as §11.2.
8. **Test-only `/__test/*` helpers live only in the test Workers** (`test/support/test-worker.ts`), stand in for the admin and the generator in the app's tests, and answer 404 unless `ENVIRONMENT` is `development` on a `*.localhost` host. Neither test config is ever deployed; the production configs point at `src/worker/index.ts`.
9. **The Workers tests run over https** (`"dev": { "local_protocol": "https" }` in the test configs): without it the local runtime rewrites the request to `http:` and the `Origin` header to match (verified with wrangler 4.138.0), which would make every CSRF check meaningless in tests.
10. **The browser tests use their own Worker config with AUTH_RL and API_RL at 1,000 per minute**, because many parallel sign-ins come from one local address; Task 5 proves the production limit of 10 returns 429 on the 11th request.
11. **`GET /api/dev/outbox` "is not registered" outside development** is implemented as a 404 decided per request (the Worker's variables are only known per request); the observable behaviour is the same.
12. **`publish_invalid` issue codes**: besides the design's `attestation_required`, `slug_missing` and `photo_ref` (the design says "codes include"), the server also returns `not_generated` (no AI draft yet), `slug_invalid` (the slug no longer passes the blocklist) and every `SiteDocument` issue with its own path. Plan 2's own refusals are answered too, never as a bare 500: `render_failed` (its `detail` is an `Issue[]`) is `422 publish_invalid` with those issues, and `integrity` with `detail.reason` `"site_changed"` (the site changed between render and write, Plan 2 decision 10) is `409 conflict`, so the owner simply tries again.
13. **Admin alert emails carry no link.** The app Worker has no `ADMIN_ORIGIN` variable (§10.3); the email names the slug and version (subject) and the business name (body, escaped). Decided by the moderator on 2026-09-25: email links stay out for v1.
14. **Draft `issues.document` leaves out paths under `facts`**, which are already listed in `issues.facts`, so the editor never shows one problem twice.
15. **`upload_limit_reached` carries `retryAfter` 86,400** (every 429 must carry one, §4.1); the caps are not time-based, so the value only tells a client not to retry at once. Daily generation caps use the seconds to the next UTC midnight; the lifetime generation cap uses 86,400 too (decision 40).
16. **Unknown version ids in the admin are 404** (the table lists 409 for a version that is not pending; an id that does not exist is not found).
17. **Production ships `GENERATION_ENABLED: "false"` and `DAILY_MODEL_LIMIT: "8"` in the app and admin too**, matching the generator (Plan 3 decisions 4 and 12; Plan 3's `apps/generator/test/config.test.ts` requires every Worker that declares them to agree, and passes with these files [verified in the replay]). The app's regeneration pre-check and the admin settings page thus agree with the job's claim until the moderator turns all three on in one commit (cross-plan check §2 item 3; Task 27 only checks the values).
18. **The editor's "Fix N issues" notice sits above both panes**, not inside the preview: on phones the preview pane is hidden while editing, and the owner must still learn the preview is stale (found by the browser test at 390 px).
19. **WebKit's Tab skips links and buttons** (as Safari does by default; Plan 1 recorded the links), so the keyboard tests use Option+Tab there, and Option+Shift+Tab to go back (the shared `tabTo` helper) [verified: a plain Shift+Tab from Cancel did not reach the dialog's other button in WebKit].
20. **`sectionHasContent` mirrors Plan 1's `hasContent`**, which `@asksite/renderer` does not export; a test compares it with every fixture's rendered page, so a Plan 1 change that breaks the mirror fails a test.
21. **A browser that cannot decode a chosen photo** (HEIC in most browsers) is refused before upload with "Please choose a JPG or PNG photo" (§8 step 1). Transparent PNGs are redrawn on white, because JPEG has no transparency.
22. **The ports are properties with function types, not methods.** TypeScript compares method parameters bivariantly, so a real function that needs more than the port promises still passes `satisfies`: with method syntax, a port whose `restore` env lacked `ROOT_DOMAIN` type-checked against Plan 2's real `restore`; with property syntax the same drift fails with `TS2322 … Property 'ROOT_DOMAIN' is missing` [verified in the replay].
23. **The production entries import each function by name.** `import * as generation` keeps every export in the bundle, including Plan 3's model SDKs that these Workers never call; named imports cut the app Worker from 1,036 KB to 494 KB and the admin from 1,049 KB to 441 KB (wrangler's dry run of the final replay, 2026-09-25: 492.63 KiB / 128.50 KiB gzip and 435.27 KiB / 113.84 KiB gzip) [verified].
24. **The admin settings answer `SettingsView`**: design §4.2 `AdminSettings` with `worstCaseDailyMicrousd: number | null`. Plan 3 answers `null` for a model with no recorded price (its decision 11), and the page then says "Unknown: no price is recorded for this model (check MODEL_PROVIDER and MODEL_ID)" instead of a made-up figure; saving still works. **(moderator, decided 2026-09-25 by M3)** `AdminSettings.worstCaseDailyMicrousd` is `number | null` in `@asksite/core`, and design §4.2's text now says `number | null` too; `SettingsView` compiles with either.
25. **The build page tries its first load again** (up to 3 tries, 1 s apart) when the connection drops or the server answers 5xx, and only then shows the error, with a "Try again" button (it used to show "Please try again" with no way to do so). The progress polls already tolerated failures. Found by the four-Worker journey, where a local-only D1 error hit exactly that request (moderator item 3).
26. **Every test that starts the local runtime is named `*.workerd.test.ts`** (Plan 2 decision 23). Plan 2's root `vitest.config.ts` globs cover `apps/*`, so the root `pnpm test` (and so CI) runs Plan 4's tests too: the pure ones in its `unit` project and these in its `workerd` project, after Plan 1's timing-sensitive tests, never alongside them. Each app also keeps its own Vitest config for `pnpm --filter @asksite/app test` (30 s per test, 120 s per hook, as the root `workerd` project). `packages/app-common` has no runtime test and runs in the root `unit` project like any package (65 tests, under 0.2 s of test time).
27. **`IP_HASH_KEY` can never be empty in production** (Plan 2 decision 21: `hashIp` throws on an empty key): it is in `secrets.required`, so wrangler refuses the deploy without it; were it ever empty, sign-in would answer `500 internal` (the shared error handler), never store an unkeyed hash.
28. **The four-Worker journey runs in one local runtime** (`apps/app/e2e-journey/server.ts`): wrangler's `createTestHarness` with the sites, app, admin and generator Workers from their own `wrangler.jsonc` and `.dev.vars.example`, one in-memory D1, R2 and queue, and a small front Worker (`router.ts`) that picks the Worker by host as the production routes do. **(moderator)** Design §10.2 runs the end-to-end tests against the four `wrangler dev` processes of §10.1; on Plan 2's `pnpm dev` this journey failed in 5 of 7 runs on local-only D1 errors (moderator item 3), and in one runtime it passed every run [verified]. Two harness limits were measured and worked around: it listens on a random port (a byte-for-byte TCP forward gives the fixed port 8789, TLS included), and it serves the static files of only the first Worker that has assets, even for requests routed to another Worker (a service binding from the front Worker serves the target's assets; the admin's are left out, so the journey drives the admin through its API, and its screens stay covered by Task 23).
29. **Plan 2's later amendments are followed** (its decisions 25, 27, 28 and 29, in its text of 2026-09-24 22:25; its 22:42 text, the one in the repository, changes only prose in its deploy task): a publish request over Plan 2's cap of 20 per site per UTC day (`publish_cap_reached`) is answered `429 rate_limited` with `Retry-After` from the error's `detail.retryAfter` (else the next UTC midnight); `AUTH_RL` is keyed on `hashIp(IP_HASH_KEY, ipRateKey(ip))`, so every address of one IPv6 /64 shares the limit; `site_not_found` from `takeDown`, `restore` or `setIndexable` is answered 404; and `restore` takes `MEDIA` and returns `missingPhotos`, which the admin's site page shows after a restore ("Site restored. 2 photos on it were deleted when it was taken down and will not show. Ask the owner to upload new photos and publish again."). The ports list the two new error codes and accept any `PublishError` constructor (Plan 4 only uses `instanceof`), so they fit Plan 2 before and after these amendments.
30. **The review's text flags check every free-text fact the page shows**, not only the examples in design §3.2 (business name, reviews, captions, alt text, service names, service-area note, licence labels): the street address, the city, each service-area place and each licence number too. Each is shown as text on our domain like a review, so a web address, an `@`, another phone number or a phishing word there is flagged for the reviewer the same way (Task 21's test covers both a flagged and an ordinary address, ZIP-code place and licence number). The text is matched the way a reader sees it: NFKC-normalised, with the Unicode full stops (`。` and the ones NFKC folds) and the spellings `[.]`, `(.)`, `(dot)`, `[dot]` and ` dot ` read as a dot; a web address is any dotted name ending in two or more letters or an `xn--` label (so `.dev`, `.ai`, `.ru` and `.top` count, and full-width `ｐａｙｐａｌ．ｃｏｍ` too); phone numbers are counted in any script's digits with any dash between the groups. The public email is shown to the reviewer as its own check line.
31. **The invite and sign-in pages take the token out of the address bar as soon as they open**, not after the button is pressed as design §5.2 words it. The token then never stays in the history, a screenshot or a shared address bar; the cost is that reloading the page before pressing the button shows "This link is not complete", and the owner opens the email link again. **(moderator)** Recorded at the review's request: safer, and different from the design's wording.
32. **Review alerts are throttled** (design §5.2 and §7.6: Resend Free's 100 emails a day are shared by every email type). A publish request emails `ADMIN_NOTIFY_EMAILS` only when it is the site's first request in an hour, and only for the first 10 such requests of the UTC day across all sites, counted in D1 from `site_versions` (no new table): 20 quick requests from one site send one alert, and whatever owners do, at most 10 alerts a day go to each admin. The review queue always lists everything; the alert only prompts a look. **(moderator)** The numbers are `ALERT_QUIET_MS` and `ALERTS_PER_DAY` in `routes/publish.ts`.
33. **A support address, `SUPPORT_EMAIL`**, is a new variable of the app and admin Workers (the design has only `abuse@` and `security@`, §10.4). Every owner page shows "Questions? Email <address>" in the same place, the footer (WCAG 3.2.6), which is what the messages "Contact us …" point to; the takedown notice names it and sets it as `Reply-To`. Task 27 forwards it with Cloudflare Email Routing, like `abuse@`. **(moderator)**
34. **The takedown notice is always emailed**, with the admin's message when there is one. The default text says only what the owner can act on (the site is offline; reply or write to the support address): the app never shows the internal reason.
35. **A leads page never ends inside one millisecond.** `before` stays the design's single number (§4.4 `nextBefore: number | null`), so when the last lead of a page shares its `created_at` with the next lead, the page also takes every other lead of that millisecond (it may then hold a few more than `limit`). Paging by `created_at` alone would otherwise skip them.
36. **Stored JSON is read leniently where a failure would lock someone out.** Stored `edits_json` that no longer passes `OwnerEdits` keeps each part that still does (the others read as empty); a stored AI draft that no longer passes `AiDraft` is still used when it has its three parts (the editor then lists the wording that today's rules refuse, which the owner can change); the admin's version detail shows a stored document even when a later Plan 1 rule refuses it, and its checks never throw. Each case logs one `stored_json_invalid` line. Without this, a future tightening of a Plan 1 rule would answer 500 on the owner's editor and the admin's review screen.
37. **Leaving a page that holds a draft saves first.** The questionnaire's and the editor's in-app links wait for the autosave and stay on the page, with a message, if it fails; any other way of leaving (the header link, the browser's Back) still sends the unsaved changes, and the next page's first load waits for that save, so it continues from the newest `rev`. Before this, a change typed less than 800 ms before leaving was dropped, and Publish could send the older draft.
38. **Changing, adding or removing a review clears "These reviews are from real customers"** in the same autosave, so the owner confirms the reviews as they are now (the FTC fake-review rule). The admin's review screen says the confirmation was given when the version was sent, which the publish route enforces. **(moderator)** Decided on 2026-09-25: no server-side reset of the flag when reviews change; the client-side reset stays.
39. **Uploads to a taken-down site are refused with `423 site_taken_down`**, like every other change to it (the design's upload row lists no 423). Deleting an upload stays allowed: it is a soft delete.
40. **Owners at the lifetime cap are told so.** `generation_cap_reached` says "tomorrow" (with `retryAfter` to the next UTC midnight) only when the daily per-site cap is the cause; when a regeneration is refused and the owner has used all 20 regenerations (`LIMITS.generationsPerOwnerTotal`) the message says so, with `retryAfter` 86,400 like the upload cap (decision 15). **(moderator)** Decided on 2026-09-25: first builds (`kind = 'first'`) do not count toward the lifetime cap and are never refused by it, so an owner at the cap still gets a first draft for a new site, and `generationsLeftTotal` counts regenerations only (Plan 3's `INSERT_JOB` and `generationAllowance`, and this plan's fake in Task 4). Task 9's route shows the lifetime message only for a regeneration (the site already has a succeeded build, Plan 3's rule for `kind`), so a first build refused by the per-site daily cap always gets "tomorrow" with `retryAfter` to the next UTC midnight, even when `generationsLeftTotal` is 0. Task 9's tests prove a first build queues at the cap while a regeneration is refused, and that a first build refused by the daily cap at 0 left in total is told "tomorrow" (Step 5 turns that test red without the kind check, then green again).

## Needs the moderator (found while writing this plan)

1. **The shipped daily model limit is 8, not 30 (money promise).** Plan 3 decision 4 found that design §12.4's "30 model calls per day (about $10 a day worst case on Opus 5.5)" is really $39.95 a day at 30, and ships `DAILY_MODEL_LIMIT: "8"` ($10.65); its cross-Worker config test requires the app and admin to ship the same value, so Plan 4 does (decision 17). The admin can raise it in Settings, where the worst case is shown. The user should confirm the cap. **Decided (M1, 2026-09-25):** "8" ships in every Worker that declares it, and design §12.4 changes to 8 model calls a day (about $10.65 worst case on Opus 5.5); the final model and limit are the user's call after Plan 3 Task 15's evaluation.
2. **`AdminSettings.worstCaseDailyMicrousd` becomes `number | null`** (Plan 3 decision 11); design §4.2 now says `number | null` too. Plan 4 answers `SettingsView` (decision 24), which compiles either way. **Decided (M3, 2026-09-25):** `number | null` in `@asksite/core`, and the design text matches; `SettingsView` stays as it is.
3. **Plan 2's `pnpm dev` (four `wrangler dev` processes on one local D1 file) fails now and then under concurrent access** [verified in this plan's replay; machine load average 5–180]. In 7 runs of the four-Worker journey on it, 5 failed on local-only D1 errors: once at start-up (`SQLITE_BUSY_RECOVERY` from the sites Worker's `workerd`), twice the app's read of a site answered `D1_ERROR: … internal error` while the generator wrote, and twice the generator's read just after claiming a job threw (Plan 3's job then reports `read_failed` and leaves the row `running` for the sweeper, 6 minutes later, so the owner waits). One run failed on a race in the test itself (fixed) and one passed. Production D1 is one service, so this is local only, but it will also bite people trying the product with `pnpm dev`. Plan 4's journey therefore runs the four Workers in one runtime (decision 28) and passed every run. Suggestion for Plan 2: run the Workers in one runtime too (the same `createTestHarness` set-up, or one Miniflare instance with a host router), or at least document the symptom. **Decided (2026-09-25):** Plan 2 Task 15 documents the symptom (`SQLITE_BUSY_RECOVERY`, `D1_ERROR … internal error` under `pnpm dev`) and the fix (restart); moving `pnpm dev` to one runtime waits until it is measured.
4. **Plan 2's CI does not run the app type checks or browser tests.** Its `pnpm test` already runs Plan 4's Vitest tests (the root globs cover `apps/*`, decision 26), but the apps are outside the root TypeScript programs, so `.github/workflows/ci.yml` should also run `pnpm --filter @asksite/app typecheck`, `pnpm --filter @asksite/admin typecheck`, `pnpm --filter @asksite/app test:e2e` and `pnpm --filter @asksite/admin test:e2e`, plus `pnpm --filter @asksite/app test:journey` if CI time allows (under a minute). **Decided (2026-09-25):** after Plan 4 merges, the two `--filter` typechecks and the two `test:e2e` runs are added to `ci.yml` as a targeted edit (not part of this plan).
5. **Decision 1 (no react-hook-form), decision 6's deploy rule and decisions 12, 13, 16, 17, 24, 28, 29, 30, 31, 32, 33, 35, 36, 38 and 39** differ from or add to the design text. **Decided (2026-09-25):** all accepted; decisions 1, 28 and 33 are to be folded into the design text.
6. **Plan 2's `pnpm dev` stops when an app cannot build yet** (before Task 24 writes its `src/worker/index.ts`). Plan 4 documents `pnpm dev --only sites,generator` for Tasks 4–23 ("Before you start"); the alternative is for Plan 2's `scripts/dev.ts` to skip an app whose `main` file does not exist yet. **Decided (2026-09-25):** keep `pnpm dev --only sites,generator`.
7. **Cross-plan: the lifetime generation cap blocks a second site's first draft.** By the design, an owner who has used `LIMITS.generationsPerOwnerTotal` (20) generations can never get a first draft for a second site (Plan 3's insert counts every generation of the owner). Plan 4 now says so plainly (decision 40); whether first builds should count is the moderator's call. **Decided (2026-09-25):** first builds do not count and are never refused by the lifetime cap (decision 40).
8. **`SUPPORT_EMAIL` needs a real mailbox** (decision 33): the user adds its Cloudflare Email Routing rule in Task 27 with `abuse@` and `security@`.

---
### Task 1: Shared package and the web-address policy

Creates `@asksite/app-common`, the code both Plan 4 Workers share. It stays free of Cloudflare binding types so the root `tsc -p .` typechecks and the root `pnpm test` runs it like any other package. First it proves the Stage 0 and Plan 1 names Plan 4 consumes exist.

**Files:**
- Create: `packages/app-common/package.json`, `packages/app-common/src/slug-policy.ts`, `packages/app-common/src/index.ts`
- Test: `packages/app-common/test/contracts.test.ts`, `packages/app-common/test/slug-policy.test.ts`
- Modify: `pnpm-lock.yaml` (by `pnpm install`)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `slugIssue` from `@asksite/core` (Stage 0, §2.8); the full Stage 0 and Plan 1 export list checked by `test/contracts.test.ts`; `obscenity` 0.4.6 (`RegExpMatcher`, `englishDataset`, `englishRecommendedTransformers`).
- Produces: `BRAND_SLUGS: ReadonlySet<string>` (213 exact brand, bank, platform, government and franchise names in slug form), `BLOCKED_WORDS: ReadonlySet<string>` (43 words), `PHISHING_WORDS: ReadonlySet<string>`, `type SlugProblem = "invalid" | "reserved" | "blocked"`, `slugProblem(slug: string): SlugProblem | null` (Stage 0 shape and reserved check first; then blocked on an exact brand match with hyphens removed, or a blocked word as any hyphen-separated part or as the whole joined slug), `slugFlags(slug: string): string[]` (reviewer hints only, in this order: `brand:<name>` for a brand used as one word, a pair of words or, for brands of 6 or more letters, inside a word; `word:<w>` for phishing words; `digits` for 4 or more digits; `profanity` when `obscenity` matches). Package entry `@asksite/app-common` (`./src/index.ts`).

- [ ] **Step 4: Check the contracts Plan 4 is built on**

This test is a prerequisite check, not TDD: it must pass before anything else is written. If it fails with missing names, stop and send the moderator the missing names; do not create them here. `Cannot find module './generated.ts'` is not a missing name: run Step 3's `pnpm build:css`.

`packages/app-common/test/contracts.test.ts`:

```ts
import * as core from "@asksite/core";
import * as renderer from "@asksite/renderer";
import * as siteCss from "@asksite/site-css";
import * as schema from "@asksite/site-schema";
import { describe, expect, it } from "vitest";

// Plan 4 is built against Stage 0 (@asksite/core, @asksite/site-css, Plan 1 amendment A6) and
// Plan 1 exactly as the design names them (§2.3, §2.8, §4.2, §4.3, §11.1), plus Plan 2's additive
// `ipRateKey` (its decision 27). If one of them lands with a different name, this fails first, with
// the missing names, instead of deep inside a route.
const USED = {
  core: [
    "AcceptInviteBody", "AiDraft", "ApproveBody", "AUDIT_ACTIONS", "Brief", "canonicalJson", "composeDocument", "CreateInviteBody",
    "DisableOwnerBody", "documentSha256", "EMPTY_EDITS", "ERROR_STATUS", "formActionUrl", "hashIp", "IndexableBody", "ipRateKey", "isId",
    "LIMITS", "liveKey", "LoginBody", "LOOKS", "mediaKey", "mediaUrl", "newId", "newToken", "OwnerEdits", "ownerEditedPaths",
    "PatchDraftBody", "photoRefIssues", "previewFormActionUrl", "PublishBody", "RejectBody", "RESERVED_SLUGS", "SECTION_IDS",
    "SetSlugBody", "SettingsBody", "sha256Hex", "siteUrl", "slugIssue", "TakedownBody", "toIssues", "TOKEN_PATTERN", "TTL",
    "VerifyLoginBody", "versionKey",
  ],
  schema: ["COPY_LIMITS", "DAYS", "Facts", "factSections", "HIDEABLE_SECTIONS", "isSafeUrl", "OwnerHidden", "SECTION_VARIANTS", "SiteDocument"],
  renderer: ["escapeAttr", "escapeText", "FONTS", "PALETTES", "render"],
  siteCss: ["SITE_CSS", "SITE_CSS_SHA256"],
} as const;

const missing = (module: Record<string, unknown>, names: readonly string[]) => names.filter((name) => module[name] === undefined);

describe("contracts Plan 4 consumes", () => {
  it("every name exists", () => {
    expect(missing(core, USED.core)).toEqual([]);
    expect(missing(schema, USED.schema)).toEqual([]);
    expect(missing(renderer, USED.renderer)).toEqual([]);
    expect(missing(siteCss, USED.siteCss)).toEqual([]);
  });

  it("the values Plan 4 relies on match the design", () => {
    expect(core.SECTION_IDS[0]).toBe("hero");
    expect([...schema.HIDEABLE_SECTIONS]).toEqual(["trust", "testimonials", "gallery", "about", "serviceArea", "faq"]);
    expect(core.LOOKS.map((look) => look.id)).toEqual(["classic", "bright", "outdoor", "bold"]);
    expect(core.slugIssue("preview")).toBe("reserved");
    expect(core.ERROR_STATUS.email_failed).toBe(502);
    expect(core.LIMITS).toMatchObject({ uploadsPerSite: 40, uploadsPerSiteTotal: 150, loginTokensPerOwnerPerHour: 5, loginTokensPerOwnerPerDay: 10 });
    expect(core.TTL).toEqual({ inviteMs: 604_800_000, loginTokenMs: 900_000, sessionMs: 2_592_000_000 });
    expect(core.EMPTY_EDITS).toEqual({ baseGenerationId: null, copy: {}, order: null, hidden: [], theme: null });
    expect([core.ipRateKey("203.0.113.9"), core.ipRateKey("2001:db8:77:1::a")]).toEqual(["203.0.113.9", "2001:db8:77:1::/64"]);
    expect(siteCss.SITE_CSS_SHA256).toMatch(/^[0-9a-f]{64}$/);
  });

  it("SiteDocument accepts owner-hidden sections (A6) and still refuses hiding the hero", () => {
    expect(schema.OwnerHidden.safeParse(["faq"]).success).toBe(true);
    expect(schema.OwnerHidden.safeParse(["hero"]).success).toBe(false);
  });
});
```

- [ ] **Step 6: Write the failing test**

`packages/app-common/test/slug-policy.test.ts`:

```ts
import { describe, expect, it } from "vitest";
import { BLOCKED_WORDS, BRAND_SLUGS, slugFlags, slugProblem } from "../src/slug-policy.ts";

describe("slugProblem", () => {
  it.each(["joes-plumbing", "austin-hvac-pros", "abc", "a1-roofing", "green-thumb-lawn-care"])("accepts %s", (slug) => {
    expect(slugProblem(slug)).toBeNull();
  });

  it("keeps the shape and reserved-word checks from @asksite/core", () => {
    expect(slugProblem("ab")).toBe("invalid");
    expect(slugProblem("Joes")).toBe("invalid");
    expect(slugProblem("joes--plumbing")).toBe("invalid");
    expect(slugProblem("-joes")).toBe("invalid");
    expect(slugProblem("admin")).toBe("reserved");
    expect(slugProblem("send")).toBe("reserved");
  });

  it.each(["paypal", "wells-fargo", "wellsfargo", "bank-of-america", "irs", "usps", "home-depot", "rotorooter", "asksite"])(
    "blocks the brand or agency name %s",
    (slug) => {
      expect(slugProblem(slug)).toBe("blocked");
    },
  );

  it.each(["fuck-plumbing", "joes-shit-hauling", "shit", "cunt", "nazi-roofing", "kkk"])("blocks the word in %s", (slug) => {
    expect(slugProblem(slug)).toBe("blocked");
  });

  // Real business and place names that a naive substring filter would block.
  it.each([
    "spic-and-span-cleaning",
    "dickson-electric",
    "cumming-hvac",
    "hancock-roofing",
    "middlesex-plumbing",
    "scunthorpe-plumbing",
    "cockrell-roofing",
    "dick-smith-plumbing",
    "pineapple-cleaning",
    "chase-hvac",
  ])("does not block the real name %s", (slug) => {
    expect(slugProblem(slug)).toBeNull();
  });

  it("stores every list entry in slug form (lower case letters and digits, no hyphen)", () => {
    for (const word of [...BRAND_SLUGS, ...BLOCKED_WORDS]) expect(word).toMatch(/^[a-z0-9]+$/);
    expect(BRAND_SLUGS.size).toBeGreaterThanOrEqual(150);
  });
});

describe("slugFlags", () => {
  it("returns nothing for an ordinary name", () => {
    expect(slugFlags("joes-plumbing")).toEqual([]);
  });

  it("flags a brand used as a word, a phishing word, digits and profanity for the human reviewer", () => {
    expect(slugFlags("chase-hvac")).toEqual(["brand:chase"]);
    expect(slugFlags("paypal-refund-help")).toEqual(["brand:paypal", "word:refund"]);
    expect(slugFlags("secure-login-plumbing")).toEqual(["word:secure", "word:login"]);
    expect(slugFlags("plumber-5125550142")).toEqual(["digits"]);
    expect(slugFlags("dickson-electric")).toEqual(["profanity"]);
  });

  it("flags a brand hidden inside one long word when the brand has 6 or more letters", () => {
    expect(slugFlags("mypaypalhelp")).toEqual(["brand:paypal"]);
    expect(slugFlags("pineapple-cleaning")).toEqual([]);
  });
});
```

- [ ] **Step 8: Write the implementation**

Blocking is exact-match only. A profanity library blocks real businesses (measured with obscenity 0.4.6: it matches `dickson-electric`, `cumming-hvac` (Cumming, GA), `cockpit-roofing`, `rapeseed-lawn`, `wankel-roofing`), so it only flags a slug for the human reviewer, who approves every site anyway.

`packages/app-common/src/slug-policy.ts`:

```ts
import { slugIssue } from "@asksite/core";
import { englishDataset, englishRecommendedTransformers, RegExpMatcher } from "obscenity";

// Web addresses are <slug>.<root>, so a slug must never pass for a bank, a big brand, a shipping
// company or a government service. Entries are in slug form without hyphens; "wells-fargo" and
// "wellsfargo" both match "wellsfargo". Only an EXACT match is blocked: "chase-hvac" can be a
// real business named after its owner, so a brand used as one word of a slug is only flagged for
// the human reviewer (slugFlags).
export const BRAND_SLUGS: ReadonlySet<string> = new Set([
  // Platforms, software and devices
  "google", "gmail", "youtube", "android", "apple", "icloud", "itunes", "iphone", "microsoft", "outlook",
  "office365", "hotmail", "windows", "xbox", "amazon", "alexa", "kindle", "facebook", "instagram", "whatsapp",
  "messenger", "meta", "twitter", "tiktok", "snapchat", "linkedin", "pinterest", "reddit", "discord", "telegram",
  "zoom", "slack", "dropbox", "adobe", "docusign", "netflix", "hulu", "disney", "spotify", "yahoo", "aol",
  "ebay", "etsy", "shopify", "wix", "squarespace", "godaddy", "wordpress", "cloudflare", "github", "openai",
  "chatgpt", "anthropic", "claude", "nvidia", "intel", "samsung", "sony", "dell", "lenovo", "roku", "uber",
  "lyft", "doordash", "grubhub", "instacart", "airbnb", "expedia", "tripadvisor",
  // Home-services marketplaces and listings
  "yelp", "nextdoor", "angi", "angieslist", "homeadvisor", "thumbtack", "houzz", "porch", "taskrabbit",
  "craigslist", "zillow", "redfin", "realtor", "trulia", "bbb", "betterbusinessbureau",
  // Payments, banks and money
  "paypal", "venmo", "zelle", "cashapp", "stripe", "visa", "mastercard", "amex", "americanexpress",
  "discover", "chase", "jpmorgan", "wellsfargo", "bankofamerica", "bofa", "citi", "citibank", "capitalone",
  "usbank", "pnc", "truist", "tdbank", "ally", "schwab", "fidelity", "vanguard", "robinhood", "coinbase",
  "binance", "kraken", "metamask", "sofi", "chime", "synchrony", "navyfederal", "usaa", "regions",
  "huntington", "keybank", "fifththird", "santander", "barclays", "hsbc", "westernunion", "moneygram",
  "turbotax", "intuit", "quickbooks", "hrblock", "creditkarma", "experian", "equifax", "transunion",
  // Retail and shipping
  "walmart", "target", "costco", "homedepot", "lowes", "bestbuy", "kroger", "walgreens", "cvs", "samsclub",
  "ikea", "wayfair", "menards", "acehardware", "truevalue", "sherwinwilliams", "usps", "ups", "fedex", "dhl",
  // Telecoms and utilities
  "att", "verizon", "tmobile", "sprint", "comcast", "xfinity", "spectrum", "centurylink", "directv",
  "dukeenergy", "pge", "coned",
  // Government
  "irs", "ssa", "socialsecurity", "medicare", "medicaid", "fema", "dmv", "usgov", "gov", "whitehouse", "fbi",
  "cia", "dhs", "uscis", "treasury", "usa",
  // National home-services franchises and equipment makers
  "rotorooter", "mrrooter", "mrelectric", "mrhandyman", "mrappliance", "servicemaster", "servpro",
  "mollymaid", "merrymaids", "terminix", "orkin", "trugreen", "onehourheating", "benjaminfranklin",
  "aireserv", "carrier", "trane", "lennox", "rheem", "goodman", "kohler", "moen", "generac",
  // Us, and words that claim to be us
  "asksite", "official", "verified", "verification",
]);

// Whole words that may never be one hyphen-separated part of a slug. Names that are also real
// surnames or places (Dickson, Cumming, Hancock) or products ("Spic and Span") are NOT here: a
// substring or profanity-library match would block real businesses, so those are only flagged.
export const BLOCKED_WORDS: ReadonlySet<string> = new Set([
  "fuck", "fucking", "fucker", "motherfucker", "shit", "shitty", "bullshit", "cunt", "bitch", "bitches",
  "asshole", "bastard", "dickhead", "cocksucker", "pussy", "twat", "wanker", "whore", "slut", "nigger",
  "nigga", "faggot", "fag", "retard", "retarded", "kike", "chink", "gook", "wetback", "tranny", "porn",
  "porno", "xxx", "rape", "rapist", "pedo", "pedophile", "nazi", "hitler", "kkk", "jizz", "dildo", "milf",
]);

/** Words scammers use to look official. Flagged for the reviewer, never blocked. */
export const PHISHING_WORDS: ReadonlySet<string> = new Set([
  "secure", "login", "signin", "verify", "account", "bank", "wallet", "password", "refund", "billing",
  "giftcard", "crypto", "update", "unlock", "support",
]);

export type SlugProblem = "invalid" | "reserved" | "blocked";

/** Why the owner may not use this web address, or null when they may. */
export function slugProblem(slug: string): SlugProblem | null {
  const issue = slugIssue(slug);
  if (issue !== null) return issue;
  const words = slug.split("-");
  const joined = words.join("");
  if (BRAND_SLUGS.has(joined)) return "blocked";
  if (words.some((word) => BLOCKED_WORDS.has(word)) || BLOCKED_WORDS.has(joined)) return "blocked";
  return null;
}

const profanity = new RegExpMatcher({ ...englishDataset.build(), ...englishRecommendedTransformers });

/** Hints for the human reviewer (ReviewChecks.slugFlags). A flag never blocks anything. */
export function slugFlags(slug: string): string[] {
  const words = slug.split("-");
  const joined = words.join("");
  const pairs = words.slice(1).map((word, i) => `${words[i]}${word}`);
  const brands = [...BRAND_SLUGS].filter(
    (brand) => words.includes(brand) || pairs.includes(brand) || (brand.length >= 6 && joined.includes(brand)),
  );
  const flags = brands.filter((brand) => brand !== joined).map((brand) => `brand:${brand}`);
  for (const word of words) if (PHISHING_WORDS.has(word)) flags.push(`word:${word}`);
  if ((slug.match(/\d/g) ?? []).length >= 4) flags.push("digits");
  if (profanity.hasMatch(words.join(" ")) || profanity.hasMatch(joined)) flags.push("profanity");
  return flags;
}
```


### Task 2: Email templates

The five emails Plan 4 sends (design §7.6): invite, magic link, review result (approved and rejected), site notice and admin alert. Plain text plus minimal HTML; every typed value is escaped with Plan 1's `escapeText`; links are built only from our origins, validated tokens and slugs.

**Files:**
- Create: `packages/app-common/src/emails.ts`
- Modify: `packages/app-common/src/index.ts`
- Test: `packages/app-common/test/emails.test.ts`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `TOKEN_PATTERN` from `@asksite/core`; `escapeAttr`, `escapeText` from `@asksite/renderer`; `isSafeUrl` from `@asksite/site-schema`.
- Produces: `interface EmailContent { subject: string; text: string; html: string }`; `cleanSubject(subject: string, max = 100): string`; `inviteEmail({ appOrigin, token })` (link `${appOrigin}/invite#${token}`, 7 days); `magicLinkEmail({ appOrigin, token })` (link `${appOrigin}/login#${token}`, 15 minutes); `reviewApprovedEmail({ appOrigin, liveUrl })`; `reviewRejectedEmail({ appOrigin, note })`; `siteNoticeEmail({ appOrigin, supportEmail, ownerMessage: string | null })` (the takedown notice: always names the support address; `supportEmail` must be a plain address, else it throws `Invalid support email`); `adminAlertEmail({ slug, versionNumber, businessName: string | null })`. Each returns `EmailContent`; the origin must be a bare https origin and the token must match `TOKEN_PATTERN`, else it throws `Invalid origin` / `Invalid token`.

```ts
import { describe, expect, it } from "vitest";
import {
  adminAlertEmail,
  cleanSubject,
  inviteEmail,
  magicLinkEmail,
  reviewApprovedEmail,
  reviewRejectedEmail,
  siteNoticeEmail,
} from "../src/emails.ts";

const APP = "https://app.asksite.example";
const TOKEN = "abcdefghijklmnopqrstuvwxyzABCDEFGHIJKLMNOPQ"; // 43 base64url characters
const XSS = `<img src=x onerror=alert(1)> "quoted" & 'single'`;

/** Every href in the HTML part. */
const hrefs = (html: string) => [...html.matchAll(/href="([^"]*)"/g)].map((m) => m[1]);

describe("cleanSubject", () => {
  it("removes CR, LF and other control characters and caps the length", () => {
    expect(cleanSubject("Hello\r\nBcc: victim@example.com\u0000!")).toBe("Hello Bcc: victim@example.com !");
    expect(cleanSubject("x".repeat(150))).toHaveLength(100);
  });
});

describe("inviteEmail", () => {
  it("links to the invite page with the token in the fragment and says it expires", () => {
    const email = inviteEmail({ appOrigin: APP, token: TOKEN });
    expect(email.subject).toBe("You're invited to set up your business website");
    expect(email.text).toContain(`${APP}/invite#${TOKEN}`);
    expect(email.text).toContain("7 days");
    expect(hrefs(email.html)).toEqual([`${APP}/invite#${TOKEN}`]);
  });

  it("refuses a malformed token or a non-https origin instead of building a bad link", () => {
    expect(() => inviteEmail({ appOrigin: APP, token: "short" })).toThrow("Invalid token");
    expect(() => inviteEmail({ appOrigin: "javascript:alert(1)", token: TOKEN })).toThrow("Invalid origin");
    expect(() => inviteEmail({ appOrigin: "http://app.asksite.example", token: TOKEN })).toThrow("Invalid origin");
  });
});

describe("magicLinkEmail", () => {
  it("links to the login page and says it lasts 15 minutes", () => {
    const email = magicLinkEmail({ appOrigin: APP, token: TOKEN });
    expect(email.subject).toBe("Your sign-in link");
    expect(email.text).toContain(`${APP}/login#${TOKEN}`);
    expect(email.text).toContain("15 minutes");
    expect(hrefs(email.html)).toEqual([`${APP}/login#${TOKEN}`]);
  });
});

describe("review result emails", () => {
  it("approved: links to the live site and the app", () => {
    const email = reviewApprovedEmail({ appOrigin: APP, liveUrl: "https://joes-plumbing.asksite.example/" });
    expect(email.subject).toBe("Your website is live");
    expect(hrefs(email.html)).toEqual(["https://joes-plumbing.asksite.example/", APP]);
  });

  it("rejected: shows the reviewer's note as escaped text, never as markup", () => {
    const email = reviewRejectedEmail({ appOrigin: APP, note: XSS });
    expect(email.subject).toBe("Your website needs a change before it goes live");
    expect(email.text).toContain(XSS);
    expect(email.html).toContain("&lt;img src=x onerror=alert(1)&gt;");
    expect(email.html).not.toContain("<img");
    expect(hrefs(email.html)).toEqual([APP]);
  });
});

describe("siteNoticeEmail", () => {
  it("includes the admin's message escaped, and always says where to ask", () => {
    const withMessage = siteNoticeEmail({ appOrigin: APP, supportEmail: "help@asksite.example", ownerMessage: XSS });
    expect(withMessage.subject).toBe("Your website has been taken offline");
    expect(withMessage.html).not.toContain("<img");
    expect(withMessage.html).toContain("&lt;img");
    const without = siteNoticeEmail({ appOrigin: APP, supportEmail: "help@asksite.example", ownerMessage: null });
    expect(without.text).toContain("If you have questions, reply to this email or write to help@asksite.example.");
    expect(hrefs(without.html)).toEqual([APP]);
    expect(() => siteNoticeEmail({ appOrigin: APP, supportEmail: "help desk", ownerMessage: null })).toThrow("Invalid support email");
  });
});

describe("adminAlertEmail", () => {
  it("names the site by its slug in the subject and escapes the business name", () => {
    const email = adminAlertEmail({ slug: "joes-plumbing", versionNumber: 3, businessName: XSS });
    expect(email.subject).toBe("Website waiting for review: joes-plumbing (version 3)");
    expect(email.html).not.toContain("<img");
    expect(email.text).toContain(XSS);
    expect(hrefs(email.html)).toEqual([]);
  });
});

describe("every template", () => {
  it("has no tracking pixel, no script and no style", () => {
    for (const email of [
      inviteEmail({ appOrigin: APP, token: TOKEN }),
      magicLinkEmail({ appOrigin: APP, token: TOKEN }),
      reviewApprovedEmail({ appOrigin: APP, liveUrl: "https://a-b.asksite.example/" }),
      reviewRejectedEmail({ appOrigin: APP, note: "Please fix the phone number." }),
      siteNoticeEmail({ appOrigin: APP, supportEmail: "help@asksite.example", ownerMessage: null }),
      adminAlertEmail({ slug: "abc", versionNumber: 1, businessName: null }),
    ]) {
      expect(email.html).not.toMatch(/<img|<script|<style|<iframe/i);
      expect(email.subject).not.toMatch(/[\r\n]/);
    }
  });
});
```

`packages/app-common/src/emails.ts`:

```ts
import { TOKEN_PATTERN } from "@asksite/core";
import { escapeAttr, escapeText } from "@asksite/renderer";
import { isSafeUrl } from "@asksite/site-schema";

// Email templates owned by Plan 4 (design §7.6). Plain text plus minimal escaped HTML, no images,
// no tracking. Every value a person typed (a business name, an admin's note) reaches the HTML only
// as escaped text. Links are built only from our own origins, validated tokens and slugs.

export interface EmailContent {
  readonly subject: string;
  readonly text: string;
  readonly html: string;
}

type Part = string | { readonly href: string; readonly label: string };

/** Removes CR, LF and every other control or invisible formatting character, then caps the length. */
export function cleanSubject(subject: string, max = 100): string {
  return subject.replace(/[\p{Cc}\p{Cf}]+/gu, " ").replace(/\s+/g, " ").trim().slice(0, max);
}

function assertOrigin(origin: string): void {
  if (!isSafeUrl(origin, ["https:"]) || new URL(origin).origin !== origin) throw new Error("Invalid origin");
}

function assertToken(token: string): void {
  if (!TOKEN_PATTERN.test(token)) throw new Error("Invalid token");
}

/** The support mailbox is shown as text and used as Reply-To, so it must be one plain address. */
function assertSupportEmail(email: string): void {
  if (!/^[a-z0-9._%+-]+@[a-z0-9-]+(?:\.[a-z0-9-]+)+$/i.test(email)) throw new Error("Invalid support email");
}

const paragraph = (text: string): string => `<p>${escapeText(text).replaceAll("\n", "<br>")}</p>`;

function build(subject: string, parts: readonly Part[]): EmailContent {
  const text = parts.map((part) => (typeof part === "string" ? part : `${part.label}: ${part.href}`)).join("\n\n");
  const body = parts
    .map((part) =>
      typeof part === "string" ? paragraph(part) : `<p><a href="${escapeAttr(part.href)}">${escapeText(part.label)}</a></p>`,
    )
    .join("\n");
  return { subject: cleanSubject(subject), text: `${text}\n`, html: `<!doctype html>\n<html lang="en"><body>\n${body}\n</body></html>\n` };
}

export function inviteEmail(input: { appOrigin: string; token: string }): EmailContent {
  assertOrigin(input.appOrigin);
  assertToken(input.token);
  const link = `${input.appOrigin}/invite#${input.token}`;
  return build("You're invited to set up your business website", [
    "Hi,",
    "You have been invited to build a website for your business. It takes about 15 minutes.",
    { href: link, label: "Set up your website" },
    `If the link does not open, copy this address into your browser: ${link}`,
    "The link works once and expires in 7 days. If you were not expecting this email, you can ignore it.",
  ]);
}

export function magicLinkEmail(input: { appOrigin: string; token: string }): EmailContent {
  assertOrigin(input.appOrigin);
  assertToken(input.token);
  const link = `${input.appOrigin}/login#${input.token}`;
  return build("Your sign-in link", [
    "Hi,",
    { href: link, label: "Sign in to your website" },
    `If the link does not open, copy this address into your browser: ${link}`,
    "The link works once and expires in 15 minutes. If you did not ask to sign in, you can ignore this email.",
  ]);
}

export function reviewApprovedEmail(input: { appOrigin: string; liveUrl: string }): EmailContent {
  assertOrigin(input.appOrigin);
  if (!isSafeUrl(input.liveUrl, ["https:"])) throw new Error("Invalid live URL");
  return build("Your website is live", [
    "Good news: we checked your website and it is now live. It can take about a minute to appear everywhere.",
    { href: input.liveUrl, label: "Open your website" },
    "You can keep editing it. Changes go live after we check them again.",
    { href: input.appOrigin, label: "Open your dashboard" },
  ]);
}

export function reviewRejectedEmail(input: { appOrigin: string; note: string }): EmailContent {
  assertOrigin(input.appOrigin);
  return build("Your website needs a change before it goes live", [
    "We checked your website and it needs a change before it can go live. Our note:",
    input.note,
    "Make the change and press Publish again.",
    { href: input.appOrigin, label: "Open your dashboard" },
  ]);
}

/** The takedown notice. The app never shows the internal reason, so this says only what the owner can act on. */
export function siteNoticeEmail(input: { appOrigin: string; supportEmail: string; ownerMessage: string | null }): EmailContent {
  assertOrigin(input.appOrigin);
  assertSupportEmail(input.supportEmail);
  return build("Your website has been taken offline", [
    "We have taken your website offline, so visitors cannot see it for now.",
    ...(input.ownerMessage === null ? [] : [input.ownerMessage]),
    `If you have questions, reply to this email or write to ${input.supportEmail}.`,
    { href: input.appOrigin, label: "Open your dashboard" },
  ]);
}

export function adminAlertEmail(input: { slug: string; versionNumber: number; businessName: string | null }): EmailContent {
  return build(`Website waiting for review: ${input.slug} (version ${input.versionNumber})`, [
    `A website is waiting for review. Business: ${input.businessName ?? "(no name yet)"}. Address: ${input.slug}. Version ${input.versionNumber}.`,
    "Open the review queue in the admin app to check it.",
  ]);
}
```

```ts
  adminAlertEmail,

  type EmailContent,
} from "./emails.ts";
export { BLOCKED_WORDS, BRAND_SLUGS, PHISHING_WORDS, slugFlags, slugProblem, type SlugProblem } from "./slug-policy.ts";
```

### Task 3: HTTP conventions, the mail port and audit rows

Everything design §4.1 asks of every API response, shared by both Workers: `ErrorBody` errors, the security headers, the `Origin` check against CSRF, size-limited JSON reading, rate-limit refusals, the §7.4 review-page headers, the `Mailer` port (design §7.6, restated so Plan 4 can be tested before Plan 2 lands) and the audit-row statement.

**Files:**
- Create: `packages/app-common/src/http.ts`, `packages/app-common/src/mail.ts`, `packages/app-common/src/audit.ts`
- Modify: `packages/app-common/src/index.ts`
- Test: `packages/app-common/test/http.test.ts`, `packages/app-common/test/audit.test.ts`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `ERROR_STATUS`, `toIssues`, `type ErrorBody`, `type ErrorCode`, `AUDIT_ACTIONS`, `LIMITS` (the draft-limit test only) from `@asksite/core`; `hono` 4.13.9 types.
- Produces: `JSON_MAX_BYTES` (256 KB); `DRAFT_JSON_MAX_BYTES` (1 MiB, for `PATCH /api/sites/:siteId/draft` only: A8c, design §4.1); `API_HEADERS`; `class ApiError extends Error { code: ErrorCode; extra: { issues?, retryAfter?, currentRev? } }`; `errorResponse(code, message, extra?)`; `logLine(fields)`; `handleError` (Hono `onError`: `ApiError` becomes its `ErrorBody`, anything else `500 internal` with the message hidden); `handleNotFound`; `apiHeaders()` middleware (sets every `API_HEADERS` entry the route did not set, adds HSTS only when the Worker's `ENVIRONMENT` is `production` (Plan 2 decision 8), and logs one line per request with hono's `routePath` helper; `c.req.routePath` is deprecated in hono 4.13.9); `requireOrigin(expected: (c) => string)` middleware (403 `forbidden` for any method but GET/HEAD from another origin); `readBytes(request, max)` (counts while reading; 413 `payload_too_large`); `readJson<T>(c, schema, max = JSON_MAX_BYTES)` (403 if not `application/json`, 400 bad JSON, 422 `validation_failed` with `issues`); `interface RateLimiter`; `rateLimit(limiter, key)` (429 `rate_limited`, `retryAfter` 60); `secondsUntilUtcMidnight(now)`; `reviewPageHeaders(root)`; `type EmailTag`, `interface OutgoingEmail`, `interface Mailer`, `trySend(mailer, email): Promise<boolean>`; `type AuditAction`, `interface SqlDatabase<Statement>`, `auditStatement(db, { at, actor, action, siteId, detail? })`.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**

`packages/app-common/test/http.test.ts`:

```ts
import { LIMITS } from "@asksite/core";
import { Hono } from "hono";
import { describe, expect, it } from "vitest";
import { z } from "zod";
import {
  API_HEADERS,
  ApiError,
  apiHeaders,
  DRAFT_JSON_MAX_BYTES,
  handleError,
  handleNotFound,
  rateLimit,
  readBytes,
  readJson,
  requireOrigin,
  reviewPageHeaders,
  secondsUntilUtcMidnight,
  type RateLimiter,
} from "../src/http.ts";

const ORIGIN = "https://app.asksite.example";

function makeApp(limiter: RateLimiter = { limit: async () => ({ success: true }) }) {
  const app = new Hono();
  app.use("/api/*", apiHeaders(), requireOrigin(() => ORIGIN));
  app.get("/api/ok", (c) => c.json({ ok: true }));
  app.post("/api/echo", async (c) => c.json(await readJson(c, z.strictObject({ name: z.string().max(5) }), 64)));
  app.post("/api/limited", async (c) => {
    await rateLimit(limiter, "key");
    return c.json({ ok: true });
  });
  app.get("/api/boom", () => {
    throw new Error("secret detail");
  });
  app.get("/api/framed", (c) => c.text("page", 200, { "X-Frame-Options": "SAMEORIGIN" }));
  app.notFound(handleNotFound);
  app.onError(handleError);
  return app;
}

const post = (path: string, body: string, headers: Record<string, string> = {}) =>
  makeApp().request(path, { method: "POST", body, headers: { Origin: ORIGIN, "Content-Type": "application/json", ...headers } });

describe("apiHeaders", () => {
  it("sets the API security headers on success, error and not-found responses", async () => {
    for (const path of ["/api/ok", "/api/boom", "/api/nope"]) {
      const res = await makeApp().request(path);
      for (const [name, value] of Object.entries(API_HEADERS)) expect(res.headers.get(name)).toBe(value);
    }
  });

  it("keeps a header the route set itself", async () => {
    const res = await makeApp().request("/api/framed");
    expect(res.headers.get("X-Frame-Options")).toBe("SAMEORIGIN");
    expect(res.headers.get("Cache-Control")).toBe("no-store");
  });

  it("sends HSTS only in production (Plan 2 decision 8: never from a localhost host)", async () => {
    const production = await makeApp().request("/api/ok", {}, { ENVIRONMENT: "production" });
    expect(production.headers.get("Strict-Transport-Security")).toBe("max-age=31536000; includeSubDomains");
    for (const env of [undefined, { ENVIRONMENT: "development" }]) {
      expect((await makeApp().request("/api/ok", {}, env)).headers.get("Strict-Transport-Security")).toBeNull();
    }
  });
});

describe("errors", () => {
  it("returns an ErrorBody with the status from ERROR_STATUS", async () => {
    const res = await makeApp().request("/api/nope");
    expect(res.status).toBe(404);
    expect(await res.json()).toEqual({ error: { code: "not_found", message: "Not found" } });
  });

  it("hides the message of an unexpected error", async () => {
    const res = await makeApp().request("/api/boom");
    expect(res.status).toBe(500);
    const body = await res.text();
    expect(body).not.toContain("secret detail");
    expect(JSON.parse(body)).toEqual({ error: { code: "internal", message: "Something went wrong. Please try again." } });
  });

  it("carries issues, currentRev and retryAfter (with the Retry-After header)", async () => {
    const app = new Hono();
    app.get("/x", () => {
      throw new ApiError("rate_limited", "Slow down", { retryAfter: 60 });
    });
    app.onError(handleError);
    const res = await app.request("/x");
    expect(res.status).toBe(429);
    expect(res.headers.get("Retry-After")).toBe("60");
    expect(await res.json()).toEqual({ error: { code: "rate_limited", message: "Slow down", retryAfter: 60 } });
  });
});

describe("requireOrigin", () => {
  it("lets GET through without an Origin", async () => {
    expect((await makeApp().request("/api/ok")).status).toBe(200);
  });

  it("refuses a state-changing request from another origin or with no Origin", async () => {
    for (const origin of ["https://evil.example", ""]) {
      const res = await makeApp().request("/api/echo", {
        method: "POST",
        body: '{"name":"a"}',
        headers: { "Content-Type": "application/json", ...(origin ? { Origin: origin } : {}) },
      });
      expect(res.status).toBe(403);
      expect(((await res.json()) as { error: { code: string } }).error.code).toBe("forbidden");
    }
  });
});

describe("readJson", () => {
  it("parses and validates a JSON body", async () => {
    const res = await post("/api/echo", '{"name":"Joe"}');
    expect(await res.json()).toEqual({ name: "Joe" });
  });

  it("refuses a body that is not declared as JSON (403, like a cross-site form)", async () => {
    const res = await post("/api/echo", '{"name":"Joe"}', { "Content-Type": "text/plain" });
    expect(res.status).toBe(403);
  });

  it("returns 413 when the body is over the limit, even without Content-Length", async () => {
    const res = await post("/api/echo", JSON.stringify({ name: "x".repeat(100) }));
    expect(res.status).toBe(413);
  });

  it("returns 400 for malformed JSON and 422 with issues for a schema failure", async () => {
    expect((await post("/api/echo", "{nope")).status).toBe(400);
    const res = await post("/api/echo", '{"name":"toolong"}');
    expect(res.status).toBe(422);
    const body = (await res.json()) as { error: { code: string; issues: Array<{ path: unknown[]; code: string }> } };
    expect(body.error.code).toBe("validation_failed");
    expect(body.error.issues[0]).toMatchObject({ path: ["name"], code: "too_big" });
  });
});

describe("DRAFT_JSON_MAX_BYTES", () => {
  it("holds the facts, brief and edits parts at their limits plus 4 KiB, so it never refuses a draft whose parts fit (A8c)", () => {
    expect(DRAFT_JSON_MAX_BYTES).toBeGreaterThanOrEqual(LIMITS.factsJsonMaxBytes + LIMITS.briefJsonMaxBytes + LIMITS.editsJsonMaxBytes + 4096);
  });
});

describe("readBytes", () => {
  it("stops reading as soon as the limit is passed", async () => {
    let pulled = 0;
    const stream = new ReadableStream<Uint8Array>({
      pull(controller) {
        pulled += 1;
        controller.enqueue(new Uint8Array(10));
        if (pulled === 100) controller.close();
      },
    });
    const request = new Request("https://x.example/", { method: "POST", body: stream, duplex: "half" } as RequestInit);
    await expect(readBytes(request, 25)).rejects.toMatchObject({ code: "payload_too_large" });
    expect(pulled).toBeLessThan(10);
  });

  it("trusts a declared Content-Length over the limit without reading", async () => {
    const request = new Request("https://x.example/", { method: "POST", body: "abc", headers: { "Content-Length": "999" } });
    await expect(readBytes(request, 10)).rejects.toMatchObject({ code: "payload_too_large" });
  });
});

describe("rateLimit", () => {
  it("maps a refused limit to 429 rate_limited with retryAfter 60", async () => {
    const res = await makeApp({ limit: async () => ({ success: false }) }).request("/api/limited", {
      method: "POST",
      headers: { Origin: ORIGIN },
    });
    expect(res.status).toBe(429);
    expect(res.headers.get("Retry-After")).toBe("60");
  });
});

describe("secondsUntilUtcMidnight", () => {
  it("counts whole seconds to the next 00:00 UTC, at least 1", () => {
    expect(secondsUntilUtcMidnight(Date.UTC(2026, 8, 24, 23, 59, 0))).toBe(60);
    expect(secondsUntilUtcMidnight(Date.UTC(2026, 8, 24, 0, 0, 0))).toBe(86_400);
    expect(secondsUntilUtcMidnight(Date.UTC(2026, 8, 24, 23, 59, 59, 999))).toBe(1);
  });
});

describe("reviewPageHeaders", () => {
  it("sandboxes the stored page and allows framing only by our own origin (§7.4)", () => {
    expect(reviewPageHeaders("asksite.example")).toEqual({
      "Content-Type": "text/html; charset=utf-8",
      "Content-Security-Policy":
        "sandbox; default-src 'none'; style-src 'unsafe-inline'; img-src https://media.asksite.example; form-action 'none'; frame-ancestors 'self'",
      "X-Frame-Options": "SAMEORIGIN",
      "X-Content-Type-Options": "nosniff",
      "X-Robots-Tag": "noindex",
      "Cache-Control": "no-store",
    });
  });
});
```
