### Task 26: Adversarial check before merging

CLAUDE.md: "Before shipping, run an adversarial check: something independent tries to break it." After Task 25 and before the moderator merges `plan4-app`, this task proves that the tests really guard the security, privacy and money rules (each mutation below must turn a test red), and then a reviewer who did not write the code tries to break the running app. Nothing is committed: every mutation is undone at once.

**Files:** none (a scratch script outside the repository; results go to the moderator).

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: Tasks 1–25.
- Produces: the mutation results and the reviewer's findings, reported to the moderator. The branch is not merged while a Critical or Important finding is open (CLAUDE.md).

- [ ] **Step 1: Start from a clean tree**

Run: `git status --short -- packages/app-common apps/app apps/admin`
Expected: prints nothing (every task is committed, so each mutation can be undone exactly).

- [ ] **Step 2: Write the mutation script (outside the repository)**

Each mutation changes one guarded line, runs the tests that must notice, and puts the file back. A mutation that does not apply is reported as `NOT APPLIED` (a stale pattern proves nothing), one that no test notices as `SURVIVED`.

`$TMPDIR/plan4-mutations.sh` (a tool, not product code: keep it out of the repository):

```bash
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Run from the repository root. Prints one line per mutation; exits 1 if any survived or did not apply.
set -u
status=0
mutate() {
  local file=$1 perl=$2 test=$3
  cp "$file" "$file.orig"
  perl -0pi -e "$perl" "$file"
  if cmp -s "$file" "$file.orig"; then echo "NOT APPLIED  $file  $perl"; status=1
  elif bash -c "$test" > /dev/null 2>&1; then echo "SURVIVED     $file  $perl"; status=1
  else echo "killed       $file  $perl"; fi
  mv "$file.orig" "$file"
}
A="pnpm --filter @asksite/app exec vitest run"
D="pnpm --filter @asksite/admin exec vitest run"
# Tokens are stored only as hashes.
mutate apps/admin/src/worker/routes/invites.ts 's/\.bind\(id, await sha256Hex\(token\), email/.bind(id, token, email/' "$D test/worker/invites.workerd.test.ts"
mutate apps/app/src/worker/routes/auth.ts 's/const sessionHash = await sha256Hex\(sessionToken\);/const sessionHash = sessionToken;/' "$A test/worker/auth.workerd.test.ts"
# Another owner's site is always 404; state changes need our Origin.
mutate apps/app/src/worker/db.ts 's/WHERE id = \? AND owner_id = \?/WHERE id = ? OR owner_id = ?/' "$A test/worker/sites.workerd.test.ts"
mutate packages/app-common/src/http.ts 's/c\.req\.header\("Origin"\) !== expected\(c\)/false/' "pnpm exec vitest run packages/app-common/test/http.test.ts"
mutate packages/app-common/src/http.ts 's/if \(isProduction\(c\) &&/if (true &&/' "pnpm exec vitest run packages/app-common/test/http.test.ts"
# Publishing: a refused address, the review-alert throttle, a frozen taken-down site.
mutate apps/app/src/worker/routes/publish.ts 's/else if \(slugProblem\(site\.slug\) !== null\)/else if (false)/' "$A test/worker/publish.workerd.test.ts"
mutate apps/app/src/worker/routes/publish.ts 's/counts\.recent === 1/counts.recent >= 1/' "$A test/worker/publish.workerd.test.ts"
mutate apps/app/src/worker/routes/uploads.ts 's/\n    assertNotTakenDown\(site\);//' "$A test/worker/uploads.workerd.test.ts"
mutate apps/app/src/worker/routes/generations.ts 's/\.generationsLeftTotal === 0/.generationsLeftTotal === -1/' "$A test/worker/generations.workerd.test.ts"
# Audit rows, text flags, lead paging, lenient stored data, comment-box issues.
mutate apps/admin/src/worker/routes/invites.ts 's/action: "invite\.revoked"/action: "invite.created"/' "$D test/worker/invites.workerd.test.ts"
mutate apps/admin/src/worker/text-flags.ts 's/const text = asRead\(typed\);/const text = typed;/' "$D test/worker/text-flags.test.ts"
mutate apps/app/src/worker/routes/leads.ts 's/if \(results\[limit\]\?\.created_at === edge\)/if (false)/' "$A test/worker/leads.workerd.test.ts"
mutate apps/app/src/worker/site-view.ts 's/edits: storedEdits\(parseStored\(site\.edits_json\)\)/edits: OwnerEdits.parse(parseStored(site.edits_json))/' "$A test/worker/sites.workerd.test.ts"
mutate apps/app/src/client/lib/draft-issues.ts 's/if \(root === "brief" && part === "comments"/if (false \&\& part === "comments"/' "$A test/client/draft-issues.test.ts"
# Only the admin publishes: the app must never bind the live bucket.
mutate apps/app/wrangler.jsonc 's/\{ "binding": "MEDIA", "bucket_name": "asksite-media" \}/{ "binding": "MEDIA", "bucket_name": "asksite-media" },\n    { "binding": "LIVE", "bucket_name": "asksite-live" }/' "$D test/worker/config.test.ts"
# A review change clears the owner's confirmation (a browser test: about two minutes).
mutate apps/app/src/client/steps/TrustStep.tsx 's/asRecord\(d\.brief\)\["reviewsAreReal"\] === true \?/false ?/' "pnpm --filter @asksite/app exec playwright test e2e/questionnaire.spec.ts --project chromium-1280 -g 'changing a review'"
exit $status
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Run it**

Run: `bash "$TMPDIR/plan4-mutations.sh"; echo "exit $?"; git status --short -- packages/app-common apps/app apps/admin`
Expected: 16 lines that each start with `killed`, then `exit 0`, and `git status` prints nothing (every file is back). A `SURVIVED` or `NOT APPLIED` line is a gap in the tests: stop and report it.

- [ ] **Step 4: A reviewer who did not write the code tries to break it**

The moderator names the reviewer (a fresh session or subagent). With the app, admin, sites and generator running locally (Task 25's journey server, `node apps/app/e2e-journey/server.ts` after `pnpm --filter @asksite/app build`, or Plan 2's `pnpm dev`), it tries at least: cross-site requests (another `Origin`, none, a `text/plain` body, a form post); every `/api/sites/:siteId/*` route with another owner's cookie; replaying and racing invite and sign-in tokens; the session cookie's flags; hostile text (`<script>`, `" onfocus=`, `javascript:` links, U+202E, zero-width characters, full-width look-alikes) in every owner field and lead field, then through the preview, the stored page, the admin review screen and every email; web addresses that dodge the blocklist; uploads (an SVG or HTML file named `.jpg`, a polyglot, 50,000,000+ pixels, an animated file, a 10 MB + 1 byte body without `Content-Length`); every rate limit and cap (sign-in, API, uploads, publish requests, review alerts, generations and the kill switch); the admin without an Access token, with another audience, and dev sign-in on a host that is not `*.localhost`; and CSP messages in both browsers. Each finding gets a severity and a reproduction; fixes follow TDD on this branch (red, then green, then every suite).

- [ ] **Step 5: Report to the moderator**

Send the moderator the script's output and the reviewer's findings. Nothing is merged while a Critical or Important finding is open (CLAUDE.md), and the merge itself is the moderator's (Global Constraints).

### Task 27: Production setup that needs the user's accounts and a real iPhone

**Needs the user:** the domain (design §12.1), the Cloudflare account with the resources Plan 2's runbook creates (D1 `asksite`, R2 `asksite-work`, `asksite-live`, `asksite-media`, the queues, DNS), a Resend account with the sending domain verified, the identity provider for Access (Google or GitHub, with MFA; §12.5), the admin email list, the chosen model (Plan 3 Task 15) and an iPhone. Every command runs on the user's machine; no key is ever pasted into chat, printed, logged or committed.

Turns the placeholders into the real values, puts the secrets in, deploys the app and admin Workers, and checks in production what could not be checked offline.

**Files:**
- Modify: `apps/app/wrangler.jsonc`, `apps/admin/wrangler.jsonc`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: Tasks 1–26.
- Produces: Production values in both `wrangler.jsonc` files; secrets in Cloudflare; deployed `asksite-app` and `asksite-admin`; the results of the production checks, reported to the moderator (who records them in `docs/journal.md`; this plan changes no file outside its own).

- [ ] **Step 1: Set the production values**

If Plan 2 Task 19 Step 4 ran after this plan merged, the domain and database id are already set in both files: check them and set the rest. In `apps/app/wrangler.jsonc` and `apps/admin/wrangler.jsonc`, replace every `asksite.example` with the chosen domain (routes, `ROOT_DOMAIN`, `APP_ORIGIN`, `ADMIN_ORIGIN`, `MAIL_FROM`, `SUPPORT_EMAIL`), set `database_id` to the id `wrangler d1 create` printed (the same in all four Workers), and set `ADMIN_NOTIFY_EMAILS` (app) and `ADMIN_EMAILS` (admin) to the admin list. Set the sender name in both `MAIL_FROM` values to the brand chosen in Plan 2 Task 19 (`$BRAND`, design §12.9), so every email carries one sender name. Do not change `MODEL_PROVIDER`, `MODEL_ID`, `DAILY_MODEL_LIMIT` or `GENERATION_ENABLED` here: the moderator changes them in the app, admin and generator together in one commit (cross-plan check §2 item 3), when the user chooses the model and limit (Plan 3 Task 15 Step 8) and when generation is turned on (Plan 3 Task 15 Step 9.4), because Plan 3's cross-Worker test requires all three Workers to match at every commit. Only check that `MODEL_PROVIDER`/`MODEL_ID` (admin), `DAILY_MODEL_LIMIT` (app and admin) and `GENERATION_ENABLED` (app and admin) equal the generator's values (the limit is chosen with the model and lands in the same combined commit; Step 2's cross-Worker test fails otherwise); if one differs, stop and ask the moderator. `GENERATION_ENABLED` stays `"false"` until the generator's model key is in and the moderator's combined commit turns it on in all three Workers. In the Cloudflare dashboard (Email, Email Routing, Routing rules) forward the `SUPPORT_EMAIL` address to the admins' inbox, as design §10.4 does for `abuse@` and `security@` (decision 33), and send it a test email that arrives.

- [ ] **Step 2: Check nothing is left as a placeholder**

Run: `grep -c 'asksite.example\|00000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000000\|"ACCESS_TEAM_DOMAIN": ""\|"ACCESS_AUD": ""\|"ADMIN_EMAILS": ""' apps/app/wrangler.jsonc apps/admin/wrangler.jsonc; pnpm --filter @asksite/app exec vitest run test/worker/config.test.ts; pnpm --filter @asksite/admin exec vitest run test/worker/config.test.ts test/worker/pricing.test.ts; pnpm exec vitest run --project unit apps/generator/test/config.test.ts; pnpm deploy:check`
Expected: `apps/app/wrangler.jsonc:0` and `apps/admin/wrangler.jsonc:0` (run it after Step 3, which fills the Access values); the config, pricing and cross-Worker tests pass; `pnpm deploy:check` prints `apps/admin: ready` and `apps/app: ready` (the other Workers as their own plans' deploy tasks leave them).

- [ ] **Step 3: Create the Cloudflare Access application (dashboard)**

In Cloudflare Zero Trust: Access, Applications, Add an application, Self-hosted. Domain `admin.<domain>`, path `*`. Policy: Allow, rule "Emails" with the admin addresses; identity provider Google or GitHub with MFA required. Save, then copy the application's "Application Audience (AUD) Tag" and the team domain (`https://<team>.cloudflareaccess.com`) into `ACCESS_AUD` and `ACCESS_TEAM_DOMAIN` in `apps/admin/wrangler.jsonc`. Keep `ADMIN_AUTH_MODE` `"access"` (the config test fails otherwise).

- [ ] **Step 4: First deploy of the app, with its secrets (wrangler refuses a new Worker's first deploy while `secrets.required` are missing and `wrangler secret put` cannot run before the Worker exists, so the secrets go in a temporary owner-only file that is deleted at once)**

Run: `bash -c 'umask 077; f=$(mktemp); trap "rm -f \"\$f\"" EXIT; trap "exit 130" INT TERM HUP; node -e "process.stdout.write(\"IP_HASH_KEY=\"+require(\"node:crypto\").randomBytes(32).toString(\"base64url\")+\"\\n\")" > "$f"; read -rs -p "Resend API key for the app: " k; echo; printf "RESEND_API_KEY=%s\n" "$k" >> "$f"; unset k; pnpm --filter @asksite/app run release --secrets-file "$f"'`
Expected: the production build ends `✓ built in …`, then wrangler uploads the Worker with its two secrets and prints the Worker name and its route `app.<domain>/*`. `IP_HASH_KEY` is random and never shown; the Resend key is typed without echo; the file is deleted when the command ends, even after Ctrl-C (the `INT` trap turns Ctrl-C into an exit so the `EXIT` trap runs: macOS's `/bin/bash` 3.2 skips an `EXIT` trap alone on Ctrl-C [verified]) (wrangler's first-deploy rule read in its source, `secrets-validation.ts`).

- [ ] **Step 5: First deploy of the admin, with its secret**

Run: `bash -c 'umask 077; f=$(mktemp); trap "rm -f \"\$f\"" EXIT; trap "exit 130" INT TERM HUP; read -rs -p "Resend API key for the admin: " k; echo; printf "RESEND_API_KEY=%s\n" "$k" > "$f"; unset k; pnpm --filter @asksite/admin run release --secrets-file "$f"'`
Expected: the build, then the Worker name and its route `admin.<domain>/*`. Later deploys are just `pnpm --filter @asksite/app run release` and `pnpm --filter @asksite/admin run release` (the secrets stay); rotate a secret with `pnpm --filter <app> exec wrangler secret put <NAME>`. Never run a bare `wrangler deploy` in an app folder: it ships the last build, even an e2e one.

- [ ] **Step 6: Check Access and the admin**

In a private window open `https://admin.<domain>/`: Access asks for the identity provider sign-in, and after it the review queue shows. `curl -s -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}' https://admin.<domain>/api/admin/me` from a terminal without an Access cookie prints `302` (Access redirects to its sign-in) or `403`, never `200`. The Worker is not reachable on `*.workers.dev` (`workers_dev` is false).

- [ ] **Step 7: Check photo processing in production (design §8 [unverified] items)**

Sign in to the app as a test owner (invite your own address). In the Photos step upload, from a phone or a desktop browser: (1) a JPEG with GPS data (any phone photo with location on), (2) a photo taken in portrait orientation, (3) an animated GIF saved as WebP or an animated PNG. For each, open the photo's address (`https://media.<domain>/<siteId>/<uploadId>.webp`) and save it. Check with `pnpm --filter @asksite/app exec node -e "const s=require('sharp');s(process.argv[1]).metadata().then(m=>console.log(m.format,m.width,m.height,m.pages??1,!!m.exif))" <file>` that each is `webp`, at most 1600 px on its long side, `1` page (still) and `false` for EXIF, and that the portrait photo is taller than wide. Then send a portrait JPEG straight to the API, skipping the browser's redraw, to learn whether Cloudflare Images applies EXIF orientation itself (the local binding does not): copy the `__Host-asksite_sid` cookie value from the browser's developer tools, then run `bash -c 'umask 077; h=$(mktemp); trap "rm -f \"\$h\"" EXIT; trap "exit 130" INT TERM HUP; read -rs -p "Session cookie value: " v; echo; printf "Cookie: __Host-asksite_sid=%s\n" "$v" > "$h"; unset v; curl -s -X POST "https://app.<domain>/api/sites/<siteId>/uploads" -H "Origin: https://app.<domain>" -H @"$h" -F "file=@portrait.jpg;type=image/jpeg"'` (paste the value at the silent prompt, never into chat; curl reads the header from the owner-only file, so the cookie never appears on a command line that other processes can see [verified: curl 8.7.1 `-H @file`]). The JSON answer's `width` and `height` show whether it was turned upright. Report the result to the moderator. If production does not rotate either, only direct API uploads are affected (the app always redraws).

- [ ] **Step 8: Check an iPhone (design §8 step 1)**

On a real iPhone in Safari, in the Photos step choose a photo from the library (HEIC is the iPhone default). Report to the moderator whether Safari hands over a JPEG (the upload succeeds) or the app says "We could not read that photo. Please choose a JPG or PNG photo." Also check the questionnaire and editor with VoiceOver on (headings, labels, errors read aloud, the preview frame's name).

- [ ] **Step 9: End-to-end smoke test in production**

With no real owners invited yet (production without invites is the staging environment, design §10.4): invite yourself, build a test site, publish it, approve it in the admin, open `https://<slug>.<domain>/`, send the contact form, check the lead email arrives at the owner address and shows in the app, then take the site down with "Also delete this site's photos" and check the page and its photos stop being served within a few minutes.

- [ ] **Step 10: Commit**

```bash
git add apps/app/wrangler.jsonc apps/admin/wrangler.jsonc
git commit -m "Set production values"
git log -1 --format='%an <%ae>%n%B'
```

## Verification record (how this plan was checked before handing it over)

- **Replay after the execution check and the security/spec review (2026-09-25).** Tasks 1–25 were replayed again, task by task, from this document's text in a fresh clone of `plan1-renderer` at `fc81127` (Plan 1 including "Fix select styling"), holding Stage 0, Plan 2 and Plan 3 exactly as their current plan texts write them (every full-file block of both texts was compared with the replayed files: identical, apart from a comment in Plan 2's `scripts/dev.ts` and the `wrangler` line its text adds to `packages/core/package.json` with `pnpm add`; Plan 3's Task 13 evaluation file was left out). Every file was written from this text, every command run, and every "Expected" line in Tasks 1–25 comes from that run: each red step failed for the stated reason and each green step passed. Where a red step's state had been overwritten by a later task (Tasks 6, 17 and 18), the red run was repeated with that task's own files. Commit steps were not run (scratch rule). The earlier replays (2026-09-24 and 2026-09-25, on `77f01b6`) are superseded by this one. Before the replay, Plans 1–3 alone passed `pnpm typecheck` and `pnpm test` (unit 57 files / 888 tests, workerd 17 files / 143 tests).
- **Counts in the replay:** `@asksite/app-common`: 65 passed (65) in 5 passed (5) files (including the contract check). The owner app Worker: 76 passed (76) in 11 passed (11) files; its client logic: 74 passed (74) in 7 passed (7) files; all of `pnpm --filter @asksite/app test`: 153 passed (153) in 18 passed (18) files. The owner app's browser suite: 99 passed (run twice; see the busy-machine note). The admin's unit and Worker tests: 50 passed (50) in 11 passed (11) files, 52 in 12 files with Task 24's pricing test; its browser suite: 2 skipped, 22 passed (the settings test runs in one project only). Task 24 runs the root `pnpm test` and `pnpm typecheck` of all four plans together: the `unit` project passed 79 files / 1,075 tests and the `workerd` project 30 files / 226 tests, the typecheck exited 0, and Plan 1's golden pages were unchanged. Task 26's 16 mutations were all killed, and every file was back afterwards.
- **Busy-machine margin:** on 2026-09-25 other sessions' browser suites ran on the same Mac (load average up to about 900), and the editor's "axe on every tab" test hit Playwright's 60 s test timeout in two of three projects while every assertion that ran had passed. The four tests that run axe or the reflow check on many screens in one test (the editor's tabs, the questionnaire sweep, the 320 px sweep and the admin sweep) therefore call `test.slow()`, which gives them 180 s [verified: Playwright 1.63.0 `types/test.d.ts`, "Slow test will be given triple the default timeout"]; every other test keeps 60 s. The keyboard-only walks call it too. In this replay, with other sessions holding the load average between 10 and 130, the first full run of the owner app's browser suite had one failure: the questionnaire sweep in Chromium at 1280 px waited more than 10 s for a step's first load ("Loading your answers…"); the same spec then passed twice in every project and the whole suite passed on its rerun (99 passed). It is recorded as a load flake, not hidden: a failure that repeats on an idle machine is a real one.
- **Four-Worker journeys:** in this replay the owner's journey passed in both projects (6 s and 11 s), the admin's journey in both (1.1 minutes each: it waits out the sites Worker's 60 s page cache after the takedown) and the kill-switch test in Chromium (4 s); nothing from the repository was left running and port 8789 was free afterwards. The owner's journey had also passed 3 of 3 separate runs in one runtime on 2026-09-24 (decision 28), after 5 of 7 failures on `pnpm dev` (moderator item 3). Those `pnpm dev` runs also showed that Plan 2's script builds and serves both Plan 4 apps as intended (each app's `build`, `wrangler dev` over https on 8787 and 8788, SPA pages and `/api/*`).
- **Browsers:** Chromium headless shell and WebKit at @playwright/test 1.63.0's revisions, at 390 and 1280 px (Chromium) and 390 px (WebKit), plus 320 px reflow checks; axe with WCAG 2.2 AA tags on every screen, before and after failed submits; no CSP message on any screen, and a red proof that the CSP check catches an off-host image in both engines.
- **Offline Cloudflare behaviour measured with wrangler 4.138.0:** `createTestHarness` under Vitest 5.0.1 (listen, `applyD1Migrations`, `getEnv`, `scheduled`); D1 `UPDATE … RETURNING`, batch rollback and SELECT results in a batch; the unique-slug error text; local Rate Limiting (the 11th auth request in a minute is 429); the queue producer; the Images binding (`info`, scale-down, WebP output, `anim: false` drops animation, EXIF and GPS dropped, EXIF orientation NOT applied); https in the harness only with `local_protocol`; jose 6.2.12 inside `workerd`; `secrets.required` makes `wrangler types` deterministic and `.dev.vars` still overrides `vars`; `vite build`/`vite preview` with the Vite plugin serve the SPA fallback, `_headers` and `/api/*`; `wrangler deploy --dry-run` on both production builds; Plan 2's `pnpm dev` serving all four Workers for the journey.
- **Plan 1 amendments A8c and A8c-2 (2026-09-25, moderator; text only, not replayed).** `PATCH /api/sites/:siteId/draft` reads its body with `DRAFT_JSON_MAX_BYTES` (1 MiB, Task 3; design §4.1's one exception), because it is the only route whose body carries a whole Facts, Brief or OwnerEdits (every other `readJson` and `c.req.json` call in this plan reads a small body or is a `/__test` helper) and the proven part limits now add up to 818,176 bytes (facts 307,200, brief 74,752, edits 436,224). Task 3's test pins `DRAFT_JSON_MAX_BYTES >= LIMITS.factsJsonMaxBytes + LIMITS.briefJsonMaxBytes + LIMITS.editsJsonMaxBytes + 4096`, so the body limit never refuses a draft whose parts fit their own limits; Task 6's facts test sends a facts part one byte over its limit and asserts the per-part message, and a body over 1 MiB gets `readBytes`' message. OwnerEdits now allows at most 12 service descriptions (one per service Facts allows), so the Words tab keys them by the current service names only (Task 13's `withServiceDescription`, used by Task 17) and Task 12 explains the count issue as "You can describe at most 12 services."; Task 12 also tells the owner that opening hours on all 7 days, or 24/7 service, allow "seven days a week". Task 3 was already built when this changed: Plan 4 adds its part as one small follow-up commit (P4-2). Test counts in Tasks 3, 6, 11–14, 18 and 24 include the new tests.

## Known limits (not verified, or deliberately out of scope)

- **Plans 2 and 3 were replayed from their plan texts, not from merged code** (their current texts, 2026-09-25; Plan 3 without its Task 13 evaluation and Task 15 live checks). If the merged code of either plan differs, Task 24's typecheck and tests are the gate: a port mismatch fails there and goes to the moderator.
- **Production-only behaviour of Cloudflare Images** [unverified]: whether it applies EXIF orientation itself (the local binding does not; the owner app's redraw does, in Chromium and WebKit), and that production strips GPS and animation like the local binding (design §8). Task 27 checks both.
- **iPhone Safari and HEIC** [unverified]: whether Safari hands a JPEG to `accept="image/jpeg,image/png,image/webp"`. Task 27 checks on a real device; if not, the owner sees "Please choose a JPG or PNG photo".
- **Cloudflare Access against the real team** [unverified]: the JWT code path is tested with a local key pair; the remote key-set URL, issuer and audience are checked in Task 27.
- **Screen-reader output** was not listened to: axe, the keyboard run and the labelled structure are automated proxies. Task 27 includes a VoiceOver pass.
- **Owner-edit strictness** follows the design (§12.8): honest phrases such as "24/7", "since 1998", "weekends" or "our customers say" are refused in wording; the message names the fact that shows them instead.
- **Spam-flagged leads** stay hidden from owners (the design's §4.4 rule); there is no way for an owner to see them.
- **Brand list upkeep:** the 213 names are a starting list; the reviewer flags cover brands inside words. Adding names later is a one-line change with the test already in place.
- **Search in the admin site list** filters the loaded page (at most 500 sites) in the browser; a server-side search can come when the pilot outgrows it.
- **Leaving a page while the autosave is failing** (decision 37): the in-app links stay and say so, but the browser's Back button or closing the tab cannot be stopped without a `beforeunload` prompt, which this plan does not add (it would also stop Playwright's navigations). The failed changes were already announced ("Your changes are not saved yet") before the owner left; the next page does not repeat it.
- **The review-alert numbers are a judgement** (decision 32: once an hour per site, 10 a day in all). They bound the admin alerts whatever owners do; the other emails (sign-in links, leads) keep their own caps, and Resend's real daily count is not read. Resend Pro, which has no daily limit, is the design's step before about 20 live sites (§7.6).
- **Text flags are hints, not proof** (decision 30): a dotted word without a space ("St.Louis") is flagged as a web address, and a scam written without a dot, an `@` or a digit is not flagged. The reviewer still reads every page.
- **Takedown speed at the edge** is Plan 2's production check (its Task 19 Step 11: pages within about 60 s, photos within 300 s); the admin journey here proves it in the local runtime only.

Each finding of the two reviews of this plan, and what was done. "Replayed" means the changed steps ran in the replay above and produced the stated output.

**Execution check**
1. Task 1 failed in a fresh worktree (`generated.ts` is gitignored): FIXED. "Before you start" and Task 1 Step 3 run `pnpm install && pnpm build:css`; Step 4 names the error. Replayed: with the file deleted the contract check fails with `Cannot find module './generated.ts'`, `pnpm install` alone does not recreate it, the new Step 3 does and the check passes.
2. `pnpm dev` breaks until Task 24: FIXED by documenting `pnpm dev --only sites,generator` ("Before you start", Tasks 4 and 19; Plan 2's `--only` is in its text) and moderator item 6 (Plan 2's `dev.ts` could skip an app whose `main` is missing). Not run: it is Plan 2's script.
3. A bare `wrangler deploy` after an e2e build targeted `asksite-app`: FIXED. The e2e Workers are `asksite-app-e2e` and `asksite-admin-e2e`, and both config tests fail on a production name in a test config. Replayed: after an e2e build a bare dry run uses `dist-e2e/asksite_app_e2e/wrangler.json` (worker `asksite-app-e2e`).
4. Plain-text tokens not caught: FIXED. Task 5 checks the session row and the login token against `sha256Hex` of what the owner holds; Task 20 checks the invite row against the emailed token. Task 26's mutations of the invite and session hashing are killed.
5. `slug_invalid` untested: FIXED (Task 10 test; mutation killed).
6. Six audit rows unchecked: FIXED. `invite.accepted`, `auth.login` (Task 5), `invite.created`, `invite.revoked` (Task 20), `owner.disabled`, `owner.enabled` (Task 22) are asserted. The check also found that disabling or enabling an unknown owner wrote an audit row before answering 404: fixed (the owner is looked up first) and tested.
7. Authorisation tests that passed before the route existed: FIXED (Tasks 6, 8, 11: the rightful owner's request comes first); Task 24's pricing test has a red run. The wrangler "still the latest" note is corrected (4.139.0 and @cloudflare/vite-plugin 1.60.0 exist; the pins stay the design's pair).

- M2 review confirmation: FIXED in the client (any review change clears the tick in the same save; the admin wording says the confirmation was given when sending, which publish enforces). A server-side reset is left to the moderator (decision 38).
- M3 alert emails: FIXED differently from the suggestion's first option: once an hour per site and at most 10 a day in all (decision 32); tested (20 requests, 1 alert; the 11th site of the day gets none).
